*`tool/late-wait.js`:* `export const keepCheckInStep = (ctx, step, opts) => takeOutOfCheck(ctx, step, { ...opts, problemWhy: (record) => importHold({ root: ctx.root, statusFolders: statusReadFolders(ctx), home: ctx.home, machine: ctx.machine, record, now: ctx.now() }).why });` (its words today, late-wait.js 130, with L3's `statusFolders`) and `export { LOCK_LOOK_MS, LOCK_WAIT_MS } from "./filing.js";`. Its results and words are today's, so reading.js and its tests do not change, and filing.js never imports unedited.js.

*`tool/edit.js`:* `findEpisode`, `finishEpisode`, `saveSpellings`, `setAsideWritten`, `resolveIn`, `stoppedWords`, `status` and `guideFacts` keep their signatures (rulings 18, 22, 28, 29, 35).

**The order in which locks are taken** (exact):
1. The launch agent's run takes its drop folder's lock (`uneditedFolder`, `lockFile(dir, runsFolder(home))`) first and holds it for the run. Nothing else takes that lock.
2. A check holds one `late-check.lock` at a time: its pending's episode's (`lateCheckLockOf(home, pending.name)`), taken with `takeLateCheckLock(home, name, "late check", alive)`, never waited for (held by a live run, the pending is put off `LOCK_AGAIN_MS`), and let go before the next pending's is taken. A `final` check is put off the same way, before it asks for that lock, while a finish is at work on its episode (`finishAtWork`: a live `finish` run holds its `episode.lock`, which `runningCommand` reads, taking none; or her yes was given within `FINISH_START_MS` and its run has not ended; ruling 14), and asks it again under the lock before it deletes an export (algorithm C, step 9). While it holds `late-check.lock`, it waits for nothing and takes no other lock (L6's move tries `episode.lock` once and puts itself off).
3. A writer takes `late-check.lock` last: after the `episode.lock` it holds as a detached run (the finish), or holding no lock at all (the in-process writers: `spell`, the spellings' hand-off, a session's hand-off, `kind … --again`, `resolve`). It takes it as "write", trying every `LOCK_LOOK_MS` for at most `LOCK_WAIT_MS`, and lets it go (`end`) once its record is written or refused and any put-back of its own is done. `restoreReads` takes and lets it go the same way, once, before the writer's refusals, and before the writer's own `takeOutOfCheck`: never both at once.
4. A lock whose holder is not running is taken over at once (detached.js `takeEpisodeLock`), by a check and by a writer alike.
5. `findEpisode`'s put-back (`ownRecordBack`, ruling 35) asks `late-check.lock` once, as "write", holding no lock (a detached run calls `findEpisode` before `runDetached` takes `episode.lock`), never waits for it, and lets it go before `findEpisode` returns.

So every wait is for a `late-check.lock`, by a run that holds at most `episode.lock`, and a holder of `late-check.lock` never waits: there is no cycle.

---

#### Rulings

The plan's rulings for L5, word for word:

0. A local import writes a `record` pending before its first record reaches Dropbox, as the finish writes its own first (ruling 1): L3's `onRecordCopy` hook gives the record's entry once its work copy is written whole and before it is cloned in, and the pending is `{ version: 1, kind: "record", id, name, folder, root, machine, entries, work, lastCopy }`, `work` the import's run folder, which keeps that work copy. A record write that then fails takes the pending away. At its check, a pending whose record never reached Dropbox (the run stopped before it) is dropped, and the stopped import is found again by its marker (L3); otherwise the first record is put back only when it is gone and no later record is there; then the pending and `work` go. The import's own work does not wait for it.
1. On a local episode the finish writes its `final` pending first, before its record: `{ version: 1, kind: "final", id, name, folder, root, machine, revision, entries, took, work, lastCopy }`, `work` its own folder in `.runs`. Then its record and hand-off; then it asks the agent to run (`ctx.kickstart()` when `!ctx.agentRunning()`) and ends; its result is written then. At the check, a pending whose revision the record in force never held is dropped: the finish died before its record. `L5-task.md` rules putting the record back, as finish.js 1106-1119 does today. `finish.checking.json` is left while the pending is there, and `checkingNow` believes the pending, not the run's lock.
2. A pending of a kind lives in `pendingFolder(home)/<kind>/<id>.json` (`record`; `final`; `original`, L6). Every pending carries `work`: a folder in `.runs` its check needs, or null. `readPendings(home)` stays the import's alone (`pending/*.json`); `readPendingsOf(home, kind)` reads one kind. `waitingStatus`, `owedFor`, `importHold` and `heldInOld` read only the import's, as they do today through `readPendings`. `mine()`'s test of what is still open, and `finishDue`'s dispatch, read every kind; `clearRuns` keeps every `work` a pending of any kind names, and skips null. `dueAt` honours a pending's `nextLook` when it has one; a pending given a `nextLook` is not marked made for the run (`checked`); and `mine()`'s still-open test leaves out a pending past its `lookUntil`, which the next run looks at once (L6 sets both fields). `finishDue` stays synchronous: every check it calls is.
3. `finishDue` makes a due `final` pending under `late-check.lock`: it reads the pending again, reads the record in force, and puts a file back only when that record lists it at the entry's bytes (as `ownLateCheck` judges); a file listed at another size, or not listed, is left. When every copy is whole and the record in force holds this revision, it deletes each original the finish took from Downloads (`took`; never one from another exports folder, D12) that is unchanged since; one changed is kept and said in the log. A problem is flagged as today (`flagProblem`, the notice, `finish.problem.json`).
4. A writer into `final/` of a local episode (a finish, `finish … reels`, the spellings' new hand-off) takes `late-check.lock` around its puts and record write, and takes the entries it replaces out of every pending of this episode (`keepCheckInStep`, moved into `filing.js` as `takeOutOfCheck`). It never waits on a check.
5. `lateCheckWaits` (the other Mac's finish still checking) stays for an episode from before the switch; a local episode never meets it (D3).
6. The import loop (`importEpisodes`) runs while any pending of this Mac is open, of any kind. After its last look it reads the pendings once more, and goes on if one came meanwhile.

The choices those leave open:

7. **A departure from ruling 2, for `final` alone: its file is `pending/final/<id>-<revision>.json`.** The coordinator accepts or rejects this before the builder starts. Two hand-offs of one episode can each owe a check within 15 minutes (a hand-off made again at once, `finish … reels` twice, a finish and then the Reels'), and one file per episode would let the second write over the first's: its exports would stay in Downloads, and a copy of the first's that Dropbox loses and the second did not replace would never be put back. Keeping `<id>.json` would need one pending merged by every writer, each entry tagged by the record that wrote it (draft A's protocol), which the review found the hardest to build and which moves every check to 15 minutes after the newest write. A revision is given twice in one case alone: a finish dies after its pending and before its record (C9), and an earlier finish's check settles its marks and puts the earlier finish's copies, its hand-off among them, back over the dead one's (W3); `handoffTarget` then gives the dead one's revision to the next hand-off writer. When that writer is a finish, its pending takes the dead one's key and is written over it (the dead one's `work` is then named by none, and goes); when it is any other writer, the dead one's pending stays, and `recordHolds` tells the new writer's record at that number from the dead finish's by its bytes (ruling 11). `record` and `original` keep ruling 2's name. `checked` holds each key with its `lastCopy` and its `told` (algorithm A), so a `record` and a `final` pending of one episode, two `final` ones, a pending written again under a key the run has made, and one a writer reopened (ruling 17), are each made in a run; and a pending read again under its lock that is no longer the one the run chose (a finish wrote its own over that key meanwhile) is let go and made at its own due, never 15 minutes early (algorithm A, step 1). Cost if wrong: one word in a file's name; P3-1's `find … -name '*.json'` counts it the same.
8. **Every new path branches on `record.local`; the import's `record` pending alone branches on a local filing (`episode.local`, which `uneditedFolder` gives as `isLocal(settings)`, and no `into`, as L3's `fileEpisode` and `afterFiling` do), since its record is still being made.** A record without `local` runs today's code line for line: its finish waits and checks in its own run, writes nothing under `pending/` and says its online-only line; `keepCheckInStep` keeps the reading's words; `stoppedWords`, the guide's checking step, `status` and `lateCheckWaits` are as today; `clearChecking` without a revision is today's. What L5 puts on a shared path is a condition false for such a record, an option an old caller never gives, or a reader that finds no kind's pending for it. Cost if wrong: an old episode stops waiting for its own check, which L8's tests and L19 catch.
9. **The `record` pending also carries `label`, the drop file (`drop`, `dropFile`) and, once its record is in Dropbox, `copied: true`; an import whose pending cannot be written fails before its record; and a record write that throws always takes its pending away.** The hook, given on a local filing with no `into` (laid over `workOn`'s `deps`), writes `{ version: 1, kind: "record", id, name, folder, root: episode.root, machine: episode.machine, label, drop: file, dropFile: arrived, entries: [{ from, to, bytes }], work, lastCopy: clock() }` without `copied`, where `folder` is `dirname(dirname(to))`, `name` is `basename(folder)`, `id` is read from `from`'s text (L3's hook is given no record) and `clock` is `deps.clock || Date.now`. A pending it cannot write is thrown from it as `Object.assign(new Error("the check of its first record could not be kept on this Mac"), { code: e.code })`, as ruling 10's `onCopy` throws: `fileEpisode` fails before the clone, and the import fails as one whose record was not written (L3: the drop file stays, and what the run wrote in `video/` goes; "try it again" imports it once `pending/` can be written). Today an import whose check cannot be kept keeps its video in the drop folder, to be imported again should Dropbox lose the record; after L3 the drop file goes once the record is written, so a first record with no check could leave the recording in `video/` with no record and nothing to import it again from. Once `fileEpisode` returns, `afterFiling` writes the pending again with `copied: true`, only when its hook wrote it, and logs (due), or, when that write fails, (not noted), which costs only this mark. A `fileEpisode` that throws after the hook wrote the pending takes the pending away, whatever is at its `to` (the review of L3): `fileLocal` throws only before its record is in place (`cloneIn` takes away what it cloned when it throws, and nothing after its `writeEpisode` throws), and its catch has already taken this run's files out of `video/`, so a record put back would list files that are gone. It is never written `copied` then, and the run folder goes with the run (`keepWork` stays false). A removal that fails is logged (not taken away) and leaves the pending without `copied` and with no note of its id in `written.json` (`writeEpisode` notes only once its clone has returned): its check drops it, with nothing put back and no notice (algorithm B, step 0); a cut-short clone left at its `to` (`cloneIn` could not take it away), whose work copy went with the run, keeps it waiting instead (step 4, (whose)), still with nothing put back. `copied` is not the check's only witness: `writeEpisode` notes rev 1 of the record's id in this Mac's `written.json` once its clone has returned, before `fileEpisode` returns, and for a first record nothing lowers that note (with no record in force, `undone` never fires and `acceptRev` is never called), so `lastWritten(home, p.id) >= 1` says its record reached Dropbox (algorithm B, step 4): an import stopped between the clone and `copied`, or whose `copied` write failed, still has a lost first record put back. The drop file is no witness: one she deleted or moved by hand while an import was stopped before its clone says nothing of the record, and ruling 0 drops such a pending. A first record that does not read and begins as the import's work copy is also taken as reached (ruling 32: it is that import's clone, cut short). While the drop file is still there as it arrived and the record is gone, no whole record holds it, so the next look imports it again (L3), and the check puts nothing back (step 6). Cost if wrong: an import fails over a `pending/` this Mac cannot write, which `keepLateCheck` passed over; and an import whose note in `written.json` could not be written, stopped between its clone and `copied`, whose record Dropbox then loses within the 15 minutes, has its pending dropped as never reached (Known limits 1).
10. **The finish writes its `final` pending inside its record's writer, so the pending holds every copy and the record's own entry before the record is cloned in, through the hook L3 added.** L3 gave `textWriter` an `onCopy` (dropbox.js 221-235, called at 228), which `fileLocal` uses for `deps.onRecordCopy` (filing.js 334); the finish uses it too, and L5 adds no hook to dropbox.js. The record goes through `textWriter({ work: join(work, "record"), keep: true, onCopy: before, … })`, whose `before(rec)` writes the pending with `record: rec` (no `copied`); then the clone; then, once `writeEpisode` returns, the pending again with `copied: true` (a write that fails is passed over: `recordHolds` finds the record). A pending that cannot be written throws from `before`, so `textWriter` puts nothing in (its work copy kept, as `keep` is given) and the finish fails as one whose record was not written, in today's words, with the reason `the check of its copies could not be kept on this Mac` (no path). A clone that fails after `before` wrote the pending takes the pending away. Cost if wrong: a finish fails over a full disk on this Mac, which it would have failed over at its record anyway.
11. **"The record in force never held this revision" is told by `copied`, else `recordHolds`, by the record file the finish wrote.** `recordHolds(pending, { isWhole })` looks first at `pending.record.to`, its record's own number (`rev`): `statSync(…, { throwIfNoEntry: false })` throws → null; there and not whole on this Mac → null; there and whole → true when `pending.record.from` is there and `sameBytes(pending.record.to, pending.record.from)`, or, when that work copy is gone, when the file reads as a record of id `id`, rev `rev` and `finished.revision === revision`; else null when the file does not read as JSON (a file cut short, as before: C14); else false (a record of another writer at that number). Not there → over the record files numbered above `rev`: `record/` cannot be listed for any reason but ENOENT → null; else true when one of them is of id `id` with `finished.revision === revision`; else null when one of them does not read as JSON (a file cut short, or one this process cannot read); else false (none there, or ENOENT). A pending with `copied` was held. One without: true → held (a finish killed between its record's clone and `copied`); false → dropped (the finish died before its clone; or another writer has its number now, given its revision again after W3: ruling 7); null → the check waits (ruling 36). Its own number is judged by bytes, not by id and revision, since after W3 another writer's record at that number can hold this revision too (C24). `written.json` is not asked: `acceptRev` lowers it after Dropbox loses a record. Cost if wrong: a finish killed between its record's clone and `copied`, whose record Dropbox then loses within the 15 minutes, is dropped as never held (Known limits 1).
12. **The finish's own record is put back** only when the pending was held (ruling 11), its file at `record.to` is not there, or is there, whole on this Mac, and does not read as a record whose `rev` is `pending.rev`, and no record numbered above `pending.rev` is there; it is put back from `record.from` (`cloneIn` when it is not there, `replaceIn` over one that does not read), before the record in force is read. A record file there that reads as a record, of any writer, is never put back over: another write may have taken that number after Dropbox lost it. But one that is not the finish's own (its bytes are not `record.from`'s, or, that work copy gone, it is not of `id` with `finished.revision === revision`) means the finish was lost from the record for good, which today's run says (finish.js 1130-1131, and its notice): it is a problem (C13), the notice is posted and the exports stay. Ruling 35 keeps a command from taking that number in the first place. A record file that is not whole on this Mac (a placeholder) is left. A copy that is gone, or a put that throws, is a problem (ruling 17). Cost if wrong: a lost record of a finish stays lost, and the guide shows the hand-off before it; or a pending whose number was taken stays with its problem, said in every run's log, until Jason puts the record right and removes it.
13. **A copy in `final/` is judged as `ownLateCheck` judges it, never by its size alone** (table J, algorithm C): listed at the entry's bytes, then there, then its size, then, unless Dropbox holds it only as a placeholder (`isWhole` false, which is not read: the launch agent cannot bring a file down), its bytes against its copy on this Mac (`sameBytes`). So a file Dropbox put back at the same size, as an older hand-off or captions of the same length, is put right. Cost if wrong: each copy is read twice under the lock, the main video too: a gigabyte takes a second or two on the Mac Studio, and a writer waits that long.
14. **A reading of ruling 3, for the coordinator to accept: "the record in force holds this revision", for an export, is "the record in force still holds the part this export was taken for".** An export `t` is deleted only when all of these hold: the check found no problem; the pending still has the entry of `final/<t.name>`, and it was listed at its bytes and judged whole; the record in force's `finished.parts[t.key]` has `file` `final/<t.name>` and `source` `{ bytes: t.snap.size, mtimeMs: t.snap.mtimeMs }`; `t.place` is "Downloads" and `dirname(t.path)` is Downloads by `sameFolder` (D12, asked again); and the file at `t.path` has `t.snap`'s size and time. A spelling, the hand-off made again from the import's captions and the Reels' hand-off keep that part, so the export still goes after any of them; a later finish that took another export for that part keeps this one. Each export is judged in a `try` of its own: one that cannot be looked at or removed is kept and said in the log, and the check still completes. Why: P3-4 step 8 spells at once after the hand-off, and with `finished.revision` equal alone the export would then never leave Downloads (on the Mac Studio, Dropbox). And a `final` check is put off, as for a held lock (`LOCK_AGAIN_MS`, said once), while a finish is at work on its episode (`finishAtWork`): a live `finish` run holds its `episode.lock`, or her yes was given less than `FINISH_START_MS` ago and its run has not ended (between the quick part's `finish.asked.json` and its run's lock there are a few seconds: node loads, and `findEpisode` runs). That finish's quick part may have named the very exports the check would delete, and its run would then fail on a missing export with a reason that is not so (that Dropbox could not bring it). The check asks it before it takes its lock (algorithm A) and again under the lock, just before the first export is looked at (algorithm C, step 9), since a yes may come while the check reads the copies: then nothing is deleted, the copies it put back stay put back, and the pending is written again with `nextLook` `LOCK_AGAIN_MS` on, so the loop waits for it and the next look is put off before the lock until the finish ends. Once the run ends, the check judges each export by the record it left. A run that died without a result puts nothing off once its minute has passed. Cost if wrong: an export left in Downloads for her to tidy; none deleted whose copy was not just judged whole; a check that waits the minutes a finish of its episode takes, and reads the copies again once when a yes came while it read them; and a quick part that looked at an export the moment before the check deleted it, with no yes yet, still meets it gone (Known limits 14).
15. **The `final` check asks the gate** (algorithm C, step 6), and while the gate holds the video nothing is put back into Dropbox and no export is deleted. When the record in force is itself gated (a session not cleared, or a video not yet answered), the pending and its `work` go: `kind … --again` as a session already took the session's own files out of the check (ruling 18), and what is left of its hand-off is never put back. When it is only held beside a session (`linksOf(pending.root).sessionsBeside(record, pending.folder)` is not empty: another version answered as a session after the hand-off, which changes nothing of this video's record or `final/`), the hold lasts only until this video is answered as a session itself (then it is gated, as above) or that session's mark is cleared (finish.js `linksOf`, 320-322; gate.js `sessionNotCleared`), after which its hand-off goes on to BRIMM Ops: so the pending stays as it is, with its work copies and its exports, and is written with a `lookUntil` (the clock, when it has none), which makes it what L6's look past its hour is: not open, and made again once at the start of each run (algorithm A, rows 1 and 7) until the hold lifts; then steps 7 to 10 are made as for any other. Each line names it as `gatedAsOf` does, with its short id (`withShortId`), the held one through `once`, once a run. A `final` pending keeps no name of its own, since the video may be a session by the time its check is made: until step 6 has let it through, every line names it `an episode (<short id>)` (algorithm A's `<who>`), and from step 7 on by `shownName` of the record in force, which names a session by its label (the plan's Global Constraints: nothing new prints more of a session than its label). Cost if wrong: a video held beside a session for good keeps its exports in Downloads and its work copies on this Mac, and its check is made at every run's start, said once a run, until Jason clears or removes the session; a lost copy of a video held beside a session is put back only once the hold lifts; and some of his log lines name an episode by its short id.
16. **An episode folder that is gone is never made again, but a local import's while this Mac holds its video; a check that puts its pending away because its folder is gone says so in one short line, posted and logged. The coordinator accepts or rejects this before the builder starts; Jason has answered its question (7 Nov: "not always, keep a short notice"):** today's finish made a gone folder's `final/` again (dropbox.js `lateCheck`, 103), found no record it could read (finish.js 1128-1131), and so flagged a problem and posted its notice. Each check (past algorithm B's step 0) first lists Episodes (`episodesFolder(pending.root)`): a list that cannot be made, Episodes itself not there included (Dropbox's folder away a moment, as when its domain is reset), → the check waits (ruling 36), and nothing is put away. A folder is gone only when Episodes is listed and no longer holds its name, or holds it as no folder. Gone, a `final` check puts nothing back and deletes no export: the pending and its `work` go. Gone, a `record` check goes on as for a lost first record while this Mac still holds the episode's `video/` (`episodeFilesFolder(home, pending.name)` is a folder): Dropbox can undo the import's making of the folder together with its record, which would leave the recording in `video/` with no record and nothing to import it again from; it makes the folder and its `record/` again only to put the record back (algorithm B, step 8), as `lateCheck` makes a missing folder today ("making a folder is not writing into a file"). With no `video/` here, it is put away. Once a check has put its pending away for a gone folder, and only then (a put-away that fails is (not put away), and the next run looks again), it posts `FOLDER_GONE(<who>)` through `deps.notify` and writes the same line to the log: a vanished folder is not always Jason's doing, and its exports stay in Downloads and its copies unchecked. The line is `The folder of <who> is gone from Episodes, so the check of its copies in Dropbox is not made. Ask Jason.` (`<who>` as algorithm A gives it: a `record` pending's label, any other's `an episode (<short id>)`, since the record that would name it is gone with the folder), and it holds no path. Any other error looking at the folder → the check waits. Every other put-back makes only `record/` or `final/` inside a folder that is there. Cost if wrong: a folder Jason deleted or renamed by hand has its `final` check dropped, as he meant, with a notice he did not need; and a local import's first record made again in Dropbox if he deleted its folder within the 15 minutes while its `video/` was still on the Mac.
17. **A problem keeps the pending, and it is made again at the start of each run, or once a writer takes an entry out of it.** A check that finds a problem logs each; for `final` it writes `finish.problem.json` (`flagProblem`); the first time (the pending has neither `problem` nor `told`) it posts the notice (`problemNotice(<shown>)` for `final`, `RECORD_LOST(label)` for `record`); the pending keeps its `work` and gets `problem` (the problems joined, with no path in them), is left out of the open ones, and is made again by `finishDue(true)`, or once a writer has taken an entry out of it. A writer's `end({ put: true })` that takes an entry out of a pending with `problem` writes it without `problem`, `lookUntil` and `nextLook`, with `wasProblem` (that problem's text) and with `told` (`ctx.now()`), and gives `reopened`: what the problem was about may be the very file the writer replaced (her hand-off made again after the notice), so the pending is open again, with no second notice (a `lookUntil` an earlier wait left on it, past, would keep a run already at work from making it until the next run's start: algorithm A, row 7). It is made at the next look, also by a run that has made it already, since `checkedKey` holds `told` (algorithm A); and a writer whose `end` gave `reopened` asks the agent to run (`askAgent`), as the finish does, so that a Mac with no run at work makes it within a minute, not at her next drop. Until it is made, `status` says the problem as found earlier, and that Studio checks again (ruling 29), as the guide still leads with it; a check that finds a problem writes `problem` and drops `wasProblem`. Whenever a `final` pending is taken away (passed, dropped, gated, folder gone), the note is taken away (`clearProblem`) when no other `final` pending of that episode (id, machine, root) has a `problem`, and that is done first, before the pending is removed (algorithm C's "put away"), so a check stopped part-way never leaves a note no pending can take away; so the check of the hand-off that replaced the lost copy takes the note away when it passes, within the run that makes it. A local finish's run neither writes nor takes away the note. Cost if wrong: the guide leads with a problem that is put right, or stops leading with one that is not; a problem found again after a writer is logged again and noted again, and is not posted twice; a pending put away whose removal then fails has its note taken away a run early.
18. **The writers that take the lock on a local episode** are every one that puts a file into `final/` or takes one out of it: `finishNow` (the finish and `finish … reels`), `finishFinished` (the spellings' hand-off and an iPhone clip's as it is), `saveSpellings`' raw branch, `finishSession`, `setAsideWritten` whenever a path it sets aside or puts in is in `final/`, and `resolveIn` for a keep of B whose file is in `final/`. Each calls `takeOutOfCheck` with the absolute paths it will put or take out, `reads` (the files in `final/` it read to make its own, or copies into `history/`, each `{ path, id }`: ruling 34; algorithms E and F say which) and `rev` (the number of the record it writes), before its first write there, and `end({ put })` once its own record is written (`put: true`) or refused (`put: false`, after any put-back of its own); and, when that `end` gives `reopened`, `askAgent` (ruling 17). A record-only write (a name, a title, an answer that sets nothing aside) takes no lock. `resolveIn` is among them because a copy is judged by its bytes (ruling 13): B kept at A's size would otherwise be put back over. A writer on an episode from before the switch takes no lock, as today. Cost if wrong: a check puts back a file a writer had just replaced or taken out of `final/`.
19. **`takeOutOfCheck` for a local record** always takes the lock, also when no pending holds any of its paths; it marks the entries among `paths` in every pending of this Mac with the record's `id` (every kind), with a mark of its own `{ pid, at, to, nonce, rev }`; a pending's `problem` does not stop it. It is `busy` only when the lock is not had in `LOCK_WAIT_MS` ("held"), an earlier writer's mark cannot be settled ("unsettled"), or a copy among its `reads` changed since the writer read it or was not whole ("restored", put back or found changed; "lost", it could not be put back: ruling 34). `end` rewrites only a pending that still carries this call's `nonce`, and one it takes an entry out of while it has a `problem` it writes with `wasProblem` and `told` in place of the `problem`, and with no `lookUntil` or `nextLook`, and gives `reopened` (ruling 17). Cost if wrong: a writer stopped by a check's problem that its own write would put right.
20. **A dead writer's mark is settled by its record's number, and by this Mac's note that it wrote it.** `settleReading` of a mark that has an integer `rev`: a marked entry stays in the check only when the record in force lists its file at the entry's bytes **and** no record numbered `rev` or above is in the folder **and** `lastWritten(home, pending.id) < rev`; every other marked entry is the writer's and is left out. `written.json` is noted only once a record's clone has returned (Evidence), and a writer marks only when `undone` let it go on, when `lastWritten` was at most the rev it read (every writer of ruling 18 asks it but `resolveIn`: Known limits 13), so a note of `rev` or above says the writer's record was written here, even when Dropbox has lost it since: its files, which she was shown, are not put back over by the earlier finish's, which would make the next hand-off writer give the revision she already took to BRIMM Ops again. Once a command has said `Dropbox undid…` and called `acceptRev`, the note is lower again and the record's number alone decides (Known limits 13). A mark with no `rev` (R1c's reading, an episode from before the switch) is settled by sizes alone, as today. A mark with a `rev` reads the record in force with `readEpisode(folder, { pastCopies: true })` when no path of `conflictedCopies(folder)` is a copy of a file in `record/` (`conflictedOf`): the record in force is then the newest whole record, copies or not, and `resolve … --keep B` of a copy in `final/` (a writer, ruling 18) is not refused by the very mark that sorting the copy out lets be settled; a conflicted copy of a record still makes it `later`. So a writer killed after its record and before `end` never has an older copy of the same size put back over its file, even when Dropbox then loses that record (S4), and one killed before its record has the earlier files put back over its half-done puts. Cost if wrong: a writer killed before its record, after which a record-only write took its number, has its half-done files left out of the check (pinned by W8; Known limits 2).
21. **The finish's order on a local episode** is algorithm E. Cost if wrong: a finish fails over its check's bookkeeping, which it would have failed over at its record anyway.
22. **Who asks `checkingNow` of a local episode:** `status` alone, while no `finish` run is at work on it, and it says the line it says today, or, once `ctx.now()` is more than five minutes past the marker's `due`, the late line (Words: that the check was due then and is made as soon as it can be); then, for a local record, the line of any run at work as today (`<name>: <command> is running.`), so both may be said, then its problem lines (ruling 29), then the results as today. A record without `local` is as today (the checking line only while a `finish` run is at work, in place of its run line). `checkingNow` believes the marker by its pending alone, not by the record's revision: after a spelling (revision 2), the check of revision 1's copies is still owed and still takes the export out of Downloads, so the line stays until that pending is put away. The late line is there because a check can wait past its time (no agent run asked for it, the 15 minutes after a wake of ruling 33, a hold beside a session, or run after run on a conflicted copy or a record that cannot be read), and a time already past says nothing of that; why it waits is not written into the pending: the log says it to Jason, and her line is the same whatever it is. `stoppedWords` gives a local record its generic words whatever runs (a local finish stops nothing once it has ended; a local reading has no check, D4), and `guideFacts` asks `checkingNow` only of a record without `local`, so `facts.checking` is null for a local video: no line under the page's step, and no " after <time>" in the Reels' step. Cost if wrong: one line in `status`.
23. **The dispatch is a table.** A pending of a kind with no check (in `CHECKS` or `deps.checks`) is not read by `mine()`, holds no run open, and is said nowhere; `clearRuns` still keeps its `work`. A check of a kind that throws (an error nobody foresaw) is caught in `finishDue`: logged by its code, made for the run, its pending left as it is for the next run, and the lock let go; the import's own check throws as today (the per-minute timer catches it there). Cost if wrong: a pending of an unknown kind waits for a newer Studio; a check that throws is retried at every run.
24. **The loop's due decision** is algorithm A's table, with `dueAt(p, woke)`, and after a kind's check the pending is read again: when it is still there, has no `problem`, and is due later than now (`dueAt(after, woke) > now`: a `nextLook`, or a new `lastCopy` as L6's move gives), it is not marked made for the run, so the loop waits for it. `dueAt` takes the later of a pending's own due and its `nextLook`, so a `nextLook` can put a look off but never bring one forward: one left on a pending from an earlier wait (an upload's, a put-off move's) is passed over once L6 writes a new `lastCopy` (a move, a put-back), which then waits its 15 minutes. A pending with `owed: true` (L6's move, ruling 3 of L6: "due at once") is due at its `lastCopy`, with no 15 minutes and no wake (ruling 33). Cost if wrong: a loop that turns with no wait, ends with a check open, or leaves L6's check of a moved recording to the agent's next run; or a moved recording checked, and deleted from the Mac, before Dropbox has had its 15 minutes.
25. **`clearRuns` asks first which runs are not running, then reads the pendings of every kind once** (`readEveryPending`), and removes a dead run's folder only when no pending names it; a `work` that is not a string names none. A writer writes its pending before it ends, so a run found dead has written every pending it will. Cost if wrong: none.
26. **Run folders:** a local import's run folder stays while its `record` pending names it (`afterFiling` keeps it once `fileEpisode` returns, as the hook is set: `keepWork = Boolean(deps.onRecordCopy)`, unedited.js 598) and goes with the pending; a filing that throws keeps none, and its pending goes with it (ruling 9); a local finish's `work` stays while its `final` pending names it, and its own `finally` removes it only when no pending was written or it was taken away. Cost if wrong: a work copy gone when its check needs it.
27. **No test reaches `launchctl`:** `setup()` (tests/episodes.js) gives the deps it passes to `contextFor` `kickstart: () => kicks.push("kickstart")`, and returns `kicks`; `agentRunning` stays `() => false`. A world that builds a ctx without `setup()` and runs a local finish must give its own. explainer.test.js gives its own `kickstart` and `t.kicks`, which replace these and keep working. Cost if wrong: none.
28. **`lateCheckWaits` is asked only of a record without `local`** (edit.js 3957 and 4057). On a local record it would give "" anyway (D3); test L15 holds it. Cost if wrong: none.
29. **`status` says a check's problem from its pending:** for a `final` pending of the episode with a `problem`, its problem (no path in it) and that the export stays in Downloads; for one with `wasProblem` and no `problem` (a writer took an entry out of it since: ruling 17), that problem as found earlier, that a change since may have put it right and Studio checks again, and that the export stays in Downloads until then (Words), in place of the checking line, which `checkingNow` does not give for it; for a `record` pending with a `problem`, by its label, whose episode has no record to name it by (algorithm B makes a problem only when its first record is gone or unreadable and no later one is there, so `readEpisode` gives NONE). `status` finds the pendings of each folder it lists by `p.name === <that folder>`, `p.machine === ctx.machine` and `resolve(p.root) === resolve(ctx.root)`, never through the record in force, which a folder with a lost first record does not have. For an episode the gate holds, the problem's own words are left out. Cost if wrong: "how far is it?" says nothing of a problem the notice told her to ask about.
30. **Ruling 4's move, and the import cycles.** `keepCheckInStep`'s body moves into filing.js as `takeOutOfCheck`; its old name stays in late-wait.js as a one-line wrapper that gives the import's problem words (`importHold`), so reading.js and its tests do not change and filing.js never imports unedited.js. filing.js comes to import finish.js (`linksOf`), finish-check.js (`problemNotice`, `flagProblem`, `clearProblem`, `clearChecking`) and finish-ended.js (`askedAt`, `endedSinceYes`); each already imports filing.js, directly or through detached.js. Every name is used only inside a function, as the codebase's other cycles are (filing.js and detached.js, filing.js and kinds.js); none may be used at a module's top level. A probe of f690ed9 (where filing.js also imports put-here.js and gate.js, L3 and L4) with the three imports added (finish-check.js' names that are there today) loaded filing.js, finish.js, finish-check.js, finish-ended.js, late-wait.js, unedited.js, switch.js, edit.js, guide.js, detached.js, reading.js, dropbox.js and put-here.js each alone, on 7 Nov. Cost if wrong: a module that fails at load, which Step 11's load check and the whole suite show at once.
31. **Pendings stay out of her folder's reach:** `pending/` and its kinds' folders are inside `~/Library/Application Support/BRIMM Video`, which her folder already refuses to Claude (places.js `gatedPlaces`, 79); nothing new is made elsewhere. Cost if wrong: none.
32. **A departure from ruling 0, for the coordinator to accept: a first record Dropbox left unreadable is put back too, not only one that is gone** (algorithm B, step 8; R6). The coordinator accepts or rejects this before the builder starts. A `0001.json` that is whole on this Mac and does not parse is no record: the episode reads as an import that has not finished (`readEpisode` gives NONE). Only this Mac writes a local episode's records (L2's gate), and only its import writes number 1, so the file is that import's record cut short or garbled. It is replaced (`replaceIn`) from the import's work copy, only when its record reached Dropbox (`copied`, this Mac's note of rev 1 in `written.json`, or the file beginning as the work copy does: step 4) and no later record is there, as one that is gone is put back; one that reached it by none of these is kept, waiting (step 4), since the re-import could never write over it. Rejected, step 8 makes it a problem (`RECORD_LOST`, the pending kept), R6 asserts that, and Jason puts it back by hand. Cost if wrong: a file in Dropbox replaced that ruling 0 did not name, which held no record that could be read.
33. **A kind's 15 minutes count from the Mac's last wake and its last start as well as from its copy. A departure from D5's "15 minutes after its copy", for the coordinator to accept before the builder starts.** The loop waits a minute at most and compares the wall clock with `dueAt` (Evidence), so a check that came due while the Mac slept (Sleep from the Apple menu, or a closed lid on a MacBook: `caffeinate -i` holds off idle sleep alone) would be made within a minute of the wake, or at the start of a run started then (`finishDue(true)`), before Dropbox has reconnected and settled; the 2026-10-14 failure came minutes after the copies, and the check takes away the export and the work copies it would put a copy back from. A Mac shut down, or restarted in the night by an update, before a check was made is the same at login: the launch agent starts with Dropbox, nothing on this Mac's disk has been uploaded or reconciled yet, and a restart is no wake (`kern.waketime` reads `sec = 0` until the Mac first sleeps). So `finishDue` and the loop's wait read `woke`, the later of when the Mac last woke from sleep and when it last started, once a look: `deps.wokeAt()` when a test gives it, else `lastWake()` (`sysctl -n kern.waketime` and `kern.boottime`), but null when `deps.clock` is given, since a test's clock is not the Mac's. `dueAt(p, woke)` counts `LATE_CHECK_MS` from the later of `p.lastCopy` and `woke` for a pending with a `kind` and no `owed`. A wake before the copy changes nothing, so a Mac that did not sleep is checked as before; a Mac woken at 08:00 makes a check due at 18:10 the evening before at 08:15. macOS sets `kern.waketime` at a dark wake (Power Nap) too, so a Mac that wakes for moments in the night makes no check then (by-hand step 3 checks it; Jason keeps Power Nap on either way: "fine, leave power nap on", 7 Nov, and Known limits 12 says what follows if it does not hold). The wake is read from the kernel, and nothing is kept in the pending: `process.hrtime` runs on through sleep on the Mac Studio's node (Evidence), so no clock in the process sees a sleep, and the kernel's answer also serves a run started after the wake. An import's own pending (no `kind`) keeps today's `dueAt` (ruling 8). The same holds for L6's look once an upload's hour is past (its `lookUntil`): at a run's start that is not yet 15 minutes after the wake or the start, it is not made at once but owed to that run, which waits for it (algorithm A's table, rows 1b, 4 and 5). Cost if wrong: a check made up to 15 minutes later than it need be after a wake or a start; or, when neither can be read, one made within a minute of a wake, as without this ruling (Known limits 12).
34. **A writer has the copies it built on judged under the lock, and is said again when one was put back or changed.** A writer reads files in `final/` before its lock (Evidence: the spelling its captions, words and hand-off; the spellings' hand-off the hand-off on file and the held Reels' captions and words; a finish a reused part's captions and words, or the Reels' hand-off on file; a set-aside the files it copies into `history/`, under its lock), and its `end({ put: true })` then takes those entries out of every check: a copy Dropbox left short, or put back older at the same size, within the 15 minutes would be built into the next revision, which BRIMM Ops posts from, and nothing would check it again. Today the finish's run holds `episode.lock` until its own check has put such a copy right, and every writer waits for that. So each passes those files as `reads`, each `{ path, id }` with the `fileId` it took just before it read the file (null for one read only under the lock). Under the lock, after settling marks (algorithm D, step 3), `takeOutOfCheck` first asks whether each read's file is still the one the writer read: one that is not (the check, or another writer's judge, put it back between the read and the lock, which a judge of the copy as it is now cannot see, since it is whole again and its pending may be gone; or Dropbox changed it) refuses the writer (`busy: "restored"`), whether or not a pending holds it: what it built on is not what is there now. Then it judges each entry among the reads of a pending of this episode as the check judges it (table J, against the record the writer read, `record.files`): one that J2, J3 or J7 calls not whole is put back from its copy on this Mac, as the check would put it back, and the writer is refused (`busy: "restored"`) with nothing marked and nothing of its own written; said again, it reads the copy put back. One that cannot be put back (its copy on this Mac is gone, or the put throws) refuses it as well (`busy: "lost"`): it would build on a copy that is not whole, and the check's notice says the same. Only `reads` are judged: what a writer replaces without reading is replaced, whatever it was. Only a held pending's entries are judged (algorithm D, step 3): a pending whose finish died before its record holds copies no record has, and its check drops it (C, step 2), so its copy is never put back over the earlier finish's. A copy that is gone, or there at another size than the record lists, would stop a writer before its lock in words that are not so (`… is not in the episode's folder … Say finish again.`, a read that throws with its path, `Dropbox is still bringing … over`, `missingWords` or `lostReelsWords`: Evidence): so each writer of a local record first calls `restoreReads` with the files in `final/` it is about to read or stand on, before those refusals, and the Reels' quick part does too, before `reelsWhy` (its `missingWords` would otherwise refuse `finish … reels` before any run, and so before (a1), could put the hand-off back); it takes the lock only when one of them is gone or at another size, judges it by table J, and refuses the writer ("restored" or "lost") when it put one back or could not; when no held pending holds it, the writer goes on to today's words. A copy at its size whose bytes no longer read (captions with no `-->`, a hand-off or words that do not parse) stops the writer before its lock, in today's words (`The hand-off could not be made again (…)`, `The hand-off could not be written: …`, or a read that throws), with nothing written; the pending's own check puts it back (J7) when it is due. Reading after the lock instead would hold it through each writer's whole build, and mark files before the writer knows which it changes. Cost if wrong: a spelling or a hand-off said a second time after Dropbox lost or changed a copy in the 15 minutes, or changed a copy between the writer's read and its lock; one whose copy cannot be put back waits for Jason, as the check's own notice says.
35. **A finish's record that Dropbox lost is put back before any command builds on the record before it.** Today the finish's run holds `episode.lock` through its 15 minutes: every writer is stopped (`stoppedWords`) until its check has put a lost record back (finish.js 1118-1127), and a record-only write that took the number meanwhile makes the run fail and post its notice (1130-1131, 1153-1166). Behind the run, a command would read the record before it as the one in force, `undone` would say `Dropbox undid this Mac's last change to <name>. Say it again.` and call `acceptRev`, and, said again, the command would write the next record under the lost one's number: a spelling built on the record before (the earlier finish's captions) and on the hand-off in `final/` (the lost finish's, whose `main` `respelledHandoffV2` keeps), so BRIMM Ops is shown a hand-off whose video is one finish's and whose captions are another's, and the finish is gone from the record for good. So `findEpisode`, once it has the one episode a command names, and before `gateOf`, calls `ownRecordBack(ctx, { name: e.name, folder: e.folder, record: e.inForce })` when `e.inForce` has `local`, and, when it gives true, reads the episode again (`episodeAt(ctx, e.name, links)`) and goes on with that. `ownRecordBack`: the `final` pendings of `readPendingsOf(ctx.home, "final")` with `id === record.id`, `machine === ctx.machine`, `resolve(root) === resolve(ctx.root)`, `rev > record.rev`, and held (`copied`, or `recordHolds(p, { isWhole: ctx.isWhole })` true); none → false, with no lock asked. Else `takeLateCheckLock(ctx.home, name, "write", ctx.alive)` once: null (a live run holds it: the check, which puts the record back itself, or a writer) or a throw → false. Under it, each such pending read again (`readPendingAt`) and still so → `putOwnRecordBack(p, { isWhole: ctx.isWhole, opts })`, `opts` the rule's options of `ctx.deps` with `log: logTo(ctx.home)`, which logs (record put back); its problems are left to the check, which flags them. Then the lock is let go (`finally`), and it gives true when any record was put back. A record it cannot put back, or one it could not reach for the lock, leaves `undone` to fire as today, and if a command then takes the number, the check finds it taken and flags it (ruling 12, C13), so a finish is never lost in silence. L6's move, which writes a record under the lock it holds, calls `putOwnRecordBack` for the same pendings first (L6's to build). Cost if wrong: a read-only command that names the episode (`show`, `status "<words>"`) puts a lost record back in Dropbox before its check would, under the same lock, by the same rule; one that meets the lock at that moment says `Dropbox undid…`, as today, and the check then flags the taken number.
36. **A check that waits looks again in the run for an hour, then at each run's start; and `resolve` asks the agent to run.** A check that waited for the next run (the (cannot tell), (whose), (folder unreadable), (later) and (unsettled) lines) would ask for no run, and on the Mac Studio the next may be her next drop, days on, while the export stays in Downloads, no copy is checked and `status` says the check is made as soon as it can be. So such a check writes its pending again with `nextLook` `WAIT_LOOK_MS` on and, when it has none, `lookUntil` `WAIT_LOOK_FOR_MS` on (algorithms B and C, "wait"): L6's machinery (ruling 24), so the run looks again every five minutes while Episodes is away a moment, Dropbox brings a record over or she sorts a conflicted copy out, and can end once the hour is past; each later run's start looks once (algorithm A, rows 1 and 7). Its line is said once a run (`once`). A hold beside a session (ruling 15) writes `lookUntil` alone, so it is looked at once a run, not every five minutes. And `resolve` on a local episode that ends 0 asks the agent to run (`askAgent`) when a `final` pending of that episode (id, machine, root) is there, since sorting a conflicted copy out is what such a check waits for. Cost if wrong: an agent run, and the Mac kept awake, an hour longer while Episodes or a record stays unreadable; a check that still waits past the hour is made at the next run, as before.

---

#### The algorithms the tests leave open (exact)

**A. `finishDue` and the loop** (`importEpisodes`; everything not named here is today's).
- `clock` is `deps.clock || Date.now` and `alive` is `deps.alive || isRunning`, as `importEpisodes` has them today; `wokeAt` is `deps.wokeAt || (deps.clock ? () => null : lastWake)` (ruling 33). `woke = wokeAt()` is read once at the top of each `finishDue` call and once before each wait of the loop.
- `checkedKey(p)` is `` `${pendingKeyOf(p)}@${p.lastCopy}@${Number.isFinite(p.told) ? p.told : 0}` ``; `checked` holds `checkedKey`s, so a pending written again under its key with another `lastCopy`, or reopened by a writer (a new `told`), is another (rulings 7 and 17). Every `sayOnce` key is the pending's key.
- `hasCheck(p)`: `!p.kind || Boolean({ ...CHECKS, ...deps.checks }[p.kind])`.
- `mine()`: `readEveryPending(home)` of this Mac (`p.machine === episode.machine`) and this root (`resolve(p.root) === resolve(episode.root)`), whose `checkedKey` is not in `checked`, and `hasCheck(p)`.
- `lookOwed`, a `Set` of `checkedKey`s, made empty beside `checked` once per `importEpisodes` call: each a pending past its `lookUntil` that the run's start could not make yet, because it was not yet due (ruling 33), and that this run owes its one look.
- `dueNow(p, atStart, now)`, the decision table (`atStart` is true for the call at the run's start, 1007, and false for every other; "owed" is `lookOwed.has(checkedKey(p))`):

| | `atStart` | owed | `p.problem` | `pastLookUntil(p, now)` | `now >= dueAt(p, woke)` | made now? |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | yes | — | any | yes | yes | yes, once |
| 1b | yes | — | any | yes | no | no: `lookOwed.add(checkedKey(p))` |
| 2 | yes | — | any | no | yes | yes |
| 3 | yes | — | any | no | no | no |
| 4 | no | yes | any | any | yes | yes, once: `lookOwed.delete(checkedKey(p))` |
| 5 | no | yes | any | any | no | no |
| 6 | no | no | yes | any | any | no |
| 7 | no | no | no | yes | any | no |
| 8 | no | no | no | no | yes | yes |
| 9 | no | no | no | no | no | no |

  For an import pending (no `lookUntil`, and `dueAt` as today) this is today's test (890): it never enters `lookOwed`.
- `finishDue(atStart)`, for each `p` of `mine()`, oldest `lastCopy` first, with `dueNow` true: `wake()`; `key = pendingKeyOf(p)`; a `final` pending of an episode a finish is at work on (`finishAtWork(home, p.name, alive, clock())`) → `putOff += 1`, `sayOnce(\`finish:${key}\`, …)` (finish at work), next, with no lock asked (ruling 14); take `late-check.lock` of `p.name` as "late check"; held by a live run → `putOff += 1`, `sayOnce(\`off:${key}\`, …)` (the import's line as today; a kind's (put off)), next. Under the lock (`try … finally` releasing it):
  1. Read it again: an import pending by `readPendings` as today (`p.id`, machine, root); a kind's, `fresh = readPendingAt(home, key)`. Gone → next (not marked). A kind's `fresh` whose `checkedKey(fresh) !== checkedKey(p)` (written again since `mine()` read it: a finish's own pending written over a dead finish's key, ruling 7; a writer's reopening, ruling 17), or whose `dueAt(fresh, woke) > clock()`, is not checked now: the lock is let go, nothing is added to `checked`, and the next pending is taken; the next `mine()` takes `fresh` at its own due. (`dueNow` is not asked again here: its rows 1b and 4 change `lookOwed`.)
  2. An import pending: today's path line for line (a `reading` mark settled, `later` marked and said once; else marked and `finishLate`), "marked" being `checked.add(checkedKey(fresh))`.
  3. A kind's: `({ ...CHECKS, ...deps.checks })[kind](fresh, { ...deps, clock, alive, once })` in a `try`, `clock` and `alive` as resolved above (the launch agent's deps carry neither) and `once = (line) => sayOnce(\`said:${key}:${line}\`, line)`. A throw → (threw), `<code>` being `e.code || "an error"`, `checkedKey(fresh)` is added to `checked`, and the next pending is taken. Otherwise go on to step 4.
  4. `after = readPendingAt(home, key)`: when `after` is there, has no `problem`, and `dueAt(after, woke) > clock()`, nothing is added to `checked`; otherwise `checkedKey(fresh)` is.
  `finishDue` gives back `putOff`, as today.
- The per-minute timer (970-982): `waitingStatus` only for `mine().filter((p) => !p.kind && !p.problem)`; its `finishDue(false)` in its try, as today.
- The loop: `stillOpen = (p) => isOpen(p, clock()) || lookOwed.has(checkedKey(p))`; `open = mine().filter(stillOpen)` (1016); the wait is `Math.min(...open.map((p) => dueAt(p, woke))) - clock()` (1029), `woke` read for it; `waitingStatus` only for open pendings with no `kind` (1031, 1045). After the last look (1017-1026): `if (!progressed && !mine().some(stillOpen)) break; continue;` (ruling 6).
- `<who>` of a kind's pending: `p.label || \`an episode (${String(p.id).slice(-6)})\``: a `record` pending's import label, and any other by its short id (ruling 15).

**B. `checkRecord(p, deps)`**, under the lock (`home = deps.episode.home`, `e = p.entries[0]`, `who = p.label || an episode (<short id>)`, `opts` the rule's options of `deps`, `clock = deps.clock`, `once = deps.once || deps.log`; each "(…)" names a log line of Words). "Put away" is: `removePending(home, p)`, then `rmSync(p.work, { recursive: true, force: true })` when `p.work` is a string; a throw → (not put away), and the pending stays (made for the run). "Wait" (ruling 36) is: `writePending(home, { ...p, nextLook: clock() + WAIT_LOOK_MS, lookUntil: Number.isFinite(p.lookUntil) ? p.lookUntil : clock() + WAIT_LOOK_FOR_MS })`, then the step's line through `once`; a throw from the write is not caught here (ruling 23).
0. Never reached (rulings 0 and 9): `p.copied !== true`, `lastWritten(home, p.id) < 1`, and `statSync(e.to, { throwIfNoEntry: false })` gives nothing (its folder, or Episodes, not there included) → put away; (stopped); done. A throw from that `statSync` → go on to step 1. Its record never reached Dropbox, whatever became of its folder: an import stopped before its clone, or one whose record write threw and whose pending could not be taken away (ruling 9), whose folder `fileLocal`'s catch took away with what the run wrote in `video/`. It is put away before the folder is looked at, so it is never said to be gone (the import's own failure was said already) and never made again.
1. The folder (ruling 16): `list = readdirSync(episodesFolder(p.root))` throws (Episodes not there included) → (cannot tell); wait; done. `list` does not hold `basename(p.folder)`: `statSync(episodeFilesFolder(home, p.name), { throwIfNoEntry: false })` is a folder → go on (step 8 makes the folder again); not there, or not a folder → put away; once it is put away, (folder gone), logged and posted (`deps.notify`); done; it throws → (cannot tell); wait; done. `list` holds it: `statSync(p.folder)` not a folder → put away; (folder gone), logged and posted, as above; done; it throws → (cannot tell); wait; done.
2. What is at `e.to`: `statSync(e.to, { throwIfNoEntry: false })` throws → (cannot tell); wait; done. Not there (its folder gone included) → "absent"; there and not whole on this Mac (`(deps.isWhole || isWhole)(e.to)` false) → "placeholder"; else read it: parses with `id === p.id` and `rev === 1` → "ours"; parses otherwise → "another"; does not parse → "unreadable".
3. "ours" or "placeholder" → put away; (whole); done.
4. Whether its record reached Dropbox (rulings 0, 9 and 32). `reached` is `p.copied === true`, or `lastWritten(home, p.id) >= 1` (`writeEpisode` notes rev 1 of that id in `written.json` once its clone has returned, and for a first record nothing lowers the note), or, for "unreadable" alone, `e.from` there and `startsAlike(e.to, e.from, statSync(e.to).size)` (the file is the import's own clone, cut short). The drop file is not asked here. Not `reached` and "unreadable" → (whose); wait; done (a file the re-import can never write over, which may be its record garbled: the work copy is kept for Jason). Not `reached` otherwise → put away; (stopped); done. (Its record did not reach Dropbox: the drop file is still there, unless she took it away by hand, no whole record holds it, and the next look imports it again by its marker, L3.)
5. "another" → put away; (another); done.
6. "absent" and `there` → put away; (again); done. `known` is `p.dropFile` being an object and `p.drop` a string; `there` is `known` and the file at `p.drop` having `p.dropFile`'s size and time (`sameFile(p.drop, { size: p.dropFile.size, mtimeMs: p.dropFile.mtimeMs })`, a throw counting as false; its inode is not asked, since Dropbox may give a file it puts back in a drop folder in Dropbox a new one; and `sameFile` counts a file as the same when no id is given, so `known` is asked first). (No whole record holds the drop file now, so the next look imports it again, into the same folder by its marker, L3; a record put back meanwhile would meet that import.)
7. "absent" or "unreadable", and `(recordNumbers(p.folder)[0] || 0) > 1` → put away; (later record); done.
8. Put it back: `e.from` not there → the problem `its first record is not in Dropbox, and its copy on this Mac is gone, so it could not be put back.`; else `mkdirSync(dirname(e.to), { recursive: true })` (which makes the episode's folder too when step 1 found it gone while its `video/` is here), then `cloneIn(e.from, e.to, opts)` when "absent", `replaceIn(e.from, e.to, opts)` when "unreadable" (ruling 32); a throw → the problem `its first record could not be put back in Dropbox (<code>).`. No problem → put away; (put back); done. A problem → (could not); the first time (no `p.problem`) `deps.notify(RECORD_LOST(who))`; `writePending(home, { ...p, problem })`, without `nextLook`; done.

**C. `checkFinal(p, deps)`**, under the lock (`home`, `opts`, `clock`, `once` and "wait" as in B; `clock` is `deps.clock`, which `finishDue` always gives; a line names the pending `<who>` (algorithm A) up to step 6, and from step 7 on `shown = shownName(read.record)` (ruling 15)). `q` is the pending as it stands: `p` up to step 4, and the settled pending from step 4 on; every write, read and removal from step 4 on is of `q`, never of `p`. "Put away" is, in this order: `clearChecking(home, q.name, q.revision)`; ruling 17's rule for the note (`clearProblem(home, q.name)` when no `final` pending of the episode (id, machine, root) other than `q`'s key has a `problem`); `removePending(home, q)`; then `rmSync(q.work, { recursive: true, force: true })` when `q.work` is a string; a throw → (not put away), and the pending stays (made for the run). The note goes first, so a check stopped part-way never leaves a note that no pending can take away (ruling 17).
1. The folder (ruling 16): `readdirSync(episodesFolder(p.root))` throws (Episodes not there included) → (folder unreadable); wait; done. The list does not hold `basename(p.folder)`, or `statSync(p.folder)` is not a folder → put away; once it is put away, (folder gone), logged and posted (`deps.notify`); done. `statSync(p.folder)` throws → (folder unreadable); wait; done.
2. Held (ruling 11): `p.copied`, else `recordHolds(p, { isWhole: deps.isWhole || isWhole })`: true → go on; false → put away; (stopped); done; null → (cannot tell); wait; done.
3. The finish's own record (ruling 12), when `p.record`: `putOwnRecordBack(p, { isWhole: deps.isWhole || isWhole, opts })`, which `ownRecordBack` uses too (ruling 35). In it, `own = p.record.to`, "none above" is `!(recordNumbers(p.folder).some((n) => n > p.rev))`, and the first that fits is done:
   - `own` not there and none above → `p.record.from` there: `mkdirSync(dirname(own), { recursive: true })`, `cloneIn(p.record.from, own, opts)`, (record put back), `put: true`; `p.record.from` not there: the problem `its record <NNNN>.json is not in Dropbox, and its copy on this Mac is gone, so it could not be put back.`
   - `own` there and not whole on this Mac (a placeholder) → nothing.
   - `own` there, whole on this Mac, not reading as a record whose `rev` is `p.rev`, and none above → `replaceIn` from `p.record.from` the same way (the same problem when it is gone).
   - `own` there, whole on this Mac, reading as a record whose `rev` is `p.rev`, and not the finish's own (`p.record.from` there and `!sameBytes(own, p.record.from)`; or `p.record.from` gone and the record not of `p.id` with `finished.revision === p.revision`) → the problem `its record <NNNN>.json was lost in Dropbox, and a later change took its number.`; nothing is put back over it.
   - Otherwise nothing (its own record, or one that does not read with a record above it).
   A throw → the problem `its record <NNNN>.json could not be put back in Dropbox (<code>).`. It gives `{ put, problems }`; `checkFinal` keeps the problems for step 8 and goes on.
4. A writer's mark: when `p.reading`, `settleReading(p, { home, log })`; `{ later }` → (unsettled); wait (of `p`, as it is); done. Else `q` is the settled pending (`settled.pending`, written back without the mark), or `p` when it had no mark.
5. The record in force: `read = readEpisode(q.folder)`; no record (NONE, UNREADABLE, CONFLICTED), `read.newer`, or `(recordNumbers(q.folder)[0] || 0) > read.record.rev` → (later); wait; done.
6. The gate (ruling 15): `links = linksOf(q.root)`; the name is `withShortId(gatedAsOf(links)(read.record), read.record)`. `gated(read.record)` → put away; (gate); done. Else `links.sessionsBeside(read.record, q.folder).length > 0` → nothing is put back and no export is deleted; `writePending(home, { ...q, lookUntil: Number.isFinite(q.lookUntil) ? q.lookUntil : clock() })` without `nextLook` (not open; made once at each run's start: algorithm A, rows 1 and 7); (held beside) through `once`; done.
7. Each entry of `q`, by table J against `listed = read.record.files || {}` (`rel(e)` is `relative(q.folder, e.to)` with "/"):
   - J1. `listed[rel(e)] !== e.bytes` → left as it is (counted for (left)).
   - J2. Not there → put back (`<what>` is `it is not there`).
   - J3. Its size is not `e.bytes` → put back (`it is <n> bytes there, not <m>`).
   - J4. Not whole on this Mac (a placeholder) → whole; it is not read.
   - J5. `e.from` is not there → whole (its size is right, and there is nothing to compare it with).
   - J6. `sameBytes(e.to, e.from)` → whole.
   - J7. Otherwise → put back (`its bytes are not the ones written`).
   Putting back: `e.from` not there → the problem `<file> in final/ is not whole in Dropbox (<what>), and its copy on this Mac is gone, so it could not be put back.`; else `mkdirSync(dirname(e.to), { recursive: true })`, then `replaceIn` (there) or `cloneIn` (not there) from `e.from`, and (put back); a throw → the problem `<file> in final/ could not be put back in Dropbox (<code>).`. Any other throw (a file that cannot be looked at, a pending that cannot be written) is not caught here: `finishDue` catches it (ruling 23). A folder of the episode that cannot be searched has already made step 5 wait (`readEpisode` gives UNREADABLE).
8. Problems (steps 3 and 7) → (problem) for each; `flagProblem(home, q.name, clock())` (a throw from it is passed over); when `q` has neither `problem` nor `told`, `deps.notify(problemNotice(shown))` (ruling 17); (kept exports) when `q.took` has any; `writePending(home, { ...q, problem: <the problems joined with " "> })`, without `wasProblem` and without `nextLook`; done. Nothing is deleted from Downloads.
9. No problem. First, under the lock, `finishAtWork(home, q.name, deps.alive, clock())` (ruling 14: a yes may have come while the copies were read) → no export is looked at or deleted, and nothing is put away (what step 7 put back stays put back); `writePending(home, { ...q, nextLook: clock() + LOCK_AGAIN_MS })`; (finish at work) through `once`; done. Else each `t` of `q.took`, in its own `try` (ruling 14; `mine` is `q`'s entry whose `to` is `join(q.folder, "final", t.name)`; `part` is `read.record.finished && read.record.finished.parts ? read.record.finished.parts[t.key] : undefined`): `statSync(t.path, { throwIfNoEntry: false })` gives nothing → nothing is said; `t.place !== "Downloads"`, or `sameFolder(dirname(t.path), downloadsFolder(home))` false → kept, (not from Downloads); no `mine`, or `mine` left by J1 → kept, (no longer holds); no `part` (a record with no finish, as a correction leaves it), or a `part` without `file === "final/" + t.name` and `source` `{ bytes: t.snap.size, mtimeMs: t.snap.mtimeMs }` → kept, (no longer holds); its size or time not `t.snap`'s → kept, (changed); else `rmSync(t.path, { recursive: true, force: true })` and (deleted). A throw → kept, (could not).
10. Put away (`q`); (passed), and (left) when any entry was left.

**D. `takeOutOfCheck` for a local record**, and its `end` (`same(p)`: `p.id === record.id && p.machine === ctx.machine && resolve(String(p.root)) === resolve(ctx.root)`; paths compared resolved and in NFC, as `keepCheckInStep` compares them):
1. Take `late-check.lock` of `name` as "write" (`takeLateCheckLock`, `ctx.alive`); while a live run holds it, `wait(LOCK_LOOK_MS)` and try again; past `LOCK_WAIT_MS` waited → `{ busy: "held" }`, nothing written.
2. Under the lock, for each pending of `readEveryPending(ctx.home)` that is `same` and has a `reading` mark: `settleReading` it; `{ later }` → let the lock go, `{ busy: "unsettled" }` (nothing else written).
3. The reads (ruling 34), before anything is marked. (a) Each read with an `id` (not null) whose file is now another (`fileId(path)` null, or its `ino`, `size` or `mtimeMs` not the id's) is changed: the copy the writer built on is not the one there now (the check, or another writer's judge, put it back between the read and the lock; or Dropbox changed it), whether or not a pending holds it. This is asked first, since a put-back below changes a file's id. (b) For each `same` `final` pending (read again) that is held (`p.copied === true`, or `recordHolds(p, { isWhole: ctx.isWhole })` true; any other is passed over here, as algorithm C, step 2, drops it or waits on it: its finish may have died before its record, and its copies are no record's), oldest `lastCopy` first, and each of its entries whose `to` is among the reads' paths: table J (algorithm C, step 7) with `listed = record.files || {}` and `ctx.isWhole` for J4; J2, J3 or J7 → put back from `e.from` as algorithm C puts back (`mkdirSync`, `replaceIn` or `cloneIn` with the rule's options of `ctx.deps`), and `log` the check's (put back) line, named by `shownName(record)`; `e.from` not there, or a put that throws → lost. (c) When any was lost: let the lock go, `{ busy: "lost" }`. Else when any was put back or changed: let the lock go, `{ busy: "restored" }`. Nothing is marked either way.
4. For each `same` pending (read again) with entries among `paths`, held or not (taking an entry out of a check never puts a file back, and a pending whose finish died is dropped at its check whatever it holds): `writePending` it with `reading: { pid: process.pid, at: ctx.now(), to: <those entries' to>, nonce: <a new random id>, rev }`. A write that throws → let the lock go and throw `Object.assign(new Error("the check of its copies could not be kept in step on this Mac"), { code: e.code })` (a mark already written is settled harmlessly by the check: no record numbered `rev` is there).
5. Give `{ end }`. `end({ put })`: for each pending marked in 4, `readPendingAt` its key; when it still carries this call's `nonce`, write it without the mark, and, with `put`, without the marked entries, and then, when it had `problem` and lost an entry, without `problem`, `lookUntil` and `nextLook`, with `wasProblem` (that `problem`) and with `told: ctx.now()` (ruling 17); a write that fails is logged (`log`, the (not taken out) line) and leaves the mark, which the check settles (ruling 20); then let the lock go. It gives `{ reopened }`: true when it wrote any pending without its `problem`. A second `end` does nothing and gives `{ reopened: false }`.

**E. The finish's order on a local episode** (`finishNow`, when `record.local`; `local` kept in the function's outer scope for its `finally`):
- (a) Everything up to "the episode changed while finish was working" (930-936), as today, but for three things on a local record, which keep `readIds`, a `Map` of path to `fileId`, declared before the `try`:
  - (a1) With `base`, before `reelsBase` (733), when `record.finished` has a string `handoff`: `busy = await restoreReads(ctx, { name: found.name, record, folder: found.folder, rels: [record.finished.handoff], rev: asked.rev + 1 }, { wait: my.wait, log: logTo(ctx.home) })`; a `busy` → `failed.push(busyWords(busy, shownName(record), { done: "nothing was copied into final/", again: sayAgain }))` and return. Then `readIds.set(<its absolute path>, fileId(<it>))`, just before `reelsBase` reads it.
  - (a2) Without `base`, at "Writing the transcripts" (795), before the loop: the reused parts are those the loop takes as `part.kept || same` (the same test, computed first); `restoreReads` with `rels` each one's `final/<stem(name)>.srt` and, when `before[part.key].words` is a string, that path; a `busy` → `failed.push(busyWords(busy, …))` as in (a1), and return.
  - (a3) In the loop, just before a reused part's captions and words are read (810-811): `readIds.set` of each. A read there that throws (the captions', 810; the words' go through `readJson`, which gives null for a file gone and throws nothing, 811), on a local record → `failed.push(busyWords("lost", shownName(record), { done: "nothing was copied into final/", again: sayAgain }))` and return: no error's message, which holds a path, reaches her (a copy no held pending holds, gone since `restoreReads` looked).
- (b) The put list `puts` (not `plan`, which `finishNow` already declares at 782), built first: for each part not reused, `[part.copy, part.name]`, then `[from, stem + ending]` for each of `.srt`, `.transcript.txt`, `.words.json` whose file is in `part.dir`, then `[from, basename(from)]` for each Story part; then `[join(work, handoffName), handoffName]`. The list `reads` (ruling 34) is `[...readIds].map(([path, id]) => ({ path, id }))`: unless `base` (so for a raw video, and for an iPhone clip finished from HyperFrames: what 810-811 read), each reused part's captions and words; with `base` (the Reels of a finished video), the hand-off on file (what `reelsBase` read). `step = await takeOutOfCheck(ctx, { name: found.name, record, paths: puts.map(([, as]) => join(finalDir, as)), reads, rev: asked.rev + 1 }, { wait: my.wait, log: logTo(ctx.home) })`. `step.busy` → `failed.push(busyWords(step.busy, shownName(record), { done: "nothing was copied into final/", again: sayAgain }))` and return: nothing is copied, and the claim is let go in `finally` as today.
- (c) The puts, walking `puts` with today's `put` (943-951); `taken` as today (963), each with `key: part.key`; the hand-off last. A put that throws: today's path (971-987), which returns; `finally` ends `step` with `put: false`.
- (d) `lastCopy` (988), the record built (990-1026) and `heldNow` (1045-1050) as today (its return also ends `step` in `finally`).
- (e) The record through `textWriter({ work: join(work, "record"), keep: true, onCopy: before, ...opts })` (1051; ruling 10: L3's `onCopy`), whose `before(rec)` writes `{ version: 1, kind: "final", id: record.id, name: found.name, folder: found.folder, root: ctx.root, machine: ctx.machine, revision, rev: asked.rev + 1, entries, took: taken, work, lastCopy, record: rec }` and then sets `pendingWritten = true`; a throw from `writePending` is thrown again as `Object.assign(new Error("the check of its copies could not be kept on this Mac"), { code: e.code })`.
- (f) `writeEpisode` returns → `writePending` with `copied: true` (a throw passed over). It throws: when `pendingWritten` (the clone failed after `before`), `removePending` (a throw passed over) and `pendingWritten = false`; then today's failure line (1061-1063), whose reason is the error's message.
- (g) `step.end({ put: recordWritten })`.
- (h) The reveal (1068); `markChecking(ctx.home, found.name, { nonce: deps.nonce, revision, due: lastCopy + LATE_CHECK_MS, took: taken.map((t) => t.place) })` in its try (1074-1081); `measured` (1084); `endBar(true)` once the record is written, whether or not the marker was.
- (i) Only when `recordWritten`: `if (!ctx.agentRunning()) ctx.kickstart()`, in one `try`; a throw adds nothing to `failed` and is said with `ctx.say` (the run's output, which a detached run writes into `brimm-video.log`), the (kick) line of Words with `shownName(nextRecord)`, as `afterJoin` says its own for a plan not kept on a Mac (join.js 623-628). A finish whose record was not written asks nothing (L11, L12).
- (j) `ended = failed.length === 0`; return the result: no wait, no check, no online-only line.
- (k) `finally`, for a local record: `step && step.end && step.end({ put: false })` (nothing once ended); no `clearChecking`, `flagProblem`, notice or `clearProblem`; `letGoClaim` as today; `endBar(ended)`; `rmSync(work)` only when `!pendingWritten`.

**F. Where each other writer takes the lock** (each only on a record with `local`; `wait` is `ctx.deps.wait`, else a real timer; `log` is `logTo(ctx.home)`, so a mark `end` could not take away, and every line `takeOutOfCheck` says, reach `brimm-video.log`; every `end` below is called only when `takeOutOfCheck` gave `{ end }`, and a second call does nothing; "ids" is a `Map` of each read's absolute path to its `fileId`, and `reads` is `[...ids].map(([path, id]) => ({ path, id }))`; "and ask" is: when the `end({ put: true })` gave `reopened`, `askAgent(ctx, shownName(<the record it wrote>))` (ruling 17)):
- `saveSpellings`, raw branch: (1) before its `gone` refusal (3986), `restoreReads(ctx, { name: found.name, record: found.record, folder: found.folder, rels: finals, rev: found.record.rev + 1 }, { wait, log })`; a `busy` → `refuse(ctx, busyWords(busy, shownName(found.record), { done: "nothing was changed", again: "Say it again in a minute." }))`. (2) After the `coming` test (3988-3992) and before the `try` whose reads are at 4013 and 4036: `ids` of every path of `finals`, absolute (`fileOf`'s, as it reads them). (3) After `lateCheckWaits` (4057, asked only without `local`) and before its puts (4059): `paths` = `writes.map(([rel]) => join(found.folder, rel))`, `reads` from `ids`, `rev` = `found.record.rev + 1`; busy → `refuse(ctx, busyWords(busy, shownName(found.record), { done: "nothing was changed", again: "Say it again in a minute." }))`; after `save` (4071) and, on a code that is not 0, after `putBack`: `end({ put: code === 0 })`, and ask; in its `catch` after `putBack`, and in its `finally`, `end({ put: false })`.
- `finishEpisode`, the Reels' quick part (`answers.reels`), when `record.finished` has a string `handoff`: before `reelsWhy` (2102), `restoreReads(ctx, { name: found.name, record, folder: found.folder, rels: [record.finished.handoff], rev: record.rev + 1 }, { wait, log })`; a `busy` → `refuse(ctx, busyWords(busy, shownName(record), { done: "nothing was changed", again: SAY_AGAIN }))`, before any claim is written. It marks nothing and takes no lock of its own; the run's (a1) and (b) do (algorithm E).
- `finishFinished`, on a local record: (1) before `coming` (1376), `restoreReads(ctx, { name: found.name, record: found.record, folder, rels: [...replaced, ...(prior ? [prior.handoff] : []), <each held Reel's final/ captions, transcript and words>], rev: found.record.rev + 1 }, { wait, log })`, a held Reel being each `prior.parts[\`reel ${n}\`]` with a string `file` (`final/<its stem>.srt`, `.transcript.txt`, and its `words` when a string); a `busy` → `{ why: busyWords(busy, who, { done: "nothing was written", again: "Say it again in a minute." }) }`.
